# Plan 5b — Plan-5 recovery: pause/resume + write-tool tightening + tests + docs + validation

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Pause / Resume", "Crisis Handling" (escalation gate), "Triggers" (scheduled), "Decay" (only as it relates to test coverage), and the relevant parts of "Multi-Message Handling" (media paths under pause).

## Background

Plan-5 (`plan-5-scheduled-jobs-decay-20260430-1337`) was force-proceeded to `done` after codex pre-emptively skipped 5 of 11 tasks in batch 4 onward. The codex executor hallucinated a read-only sandbox and refused before attempting the work. The root cause has been fixed in megaplan (`workers.py` → `_codex_exec_mode_flags` now always returns `--full-auto` for non-execute phases, and execute on macOS already had it). With that fix in place, this plan re-runs the five skipped tasks against the existing repo state.

**The work that landed in plan-5 (do not redo):** migration `0004_plan5_scheduled_jobs.sql`, `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, and the recency-weighted query/sort updates in read tools and hot context.

**The five tasks below were skipped and must land here.** Descriptions are pulled verbatim from plan-5's `finalize.json` so wording matches the original briefing.

## Scope (in)

### T1 — Global pause/resume (was plan-5 T5)
Implement global pause/resume: add `app/services/system_state.py`, make `paused_for_user` DB-backed without breaking all existing callers, add `send_outbound(..., ignore_pause=False)` and use `ignore_pause=True` for pause confirmations, detect literal `/pause` and `/resume` after inbound persistence, supersede all pending user-facing jobs on pause, clear pause and restore weekly summaries on resume, and ensure text, document, voice, and image inbound paths persist but do not enqueue while paused.

### T2 — Write-tool tightening (was plan-5 T7)
Tighten write-tool contracts: keep `schedule_checkin` transactional and compatible with the partial unique constraint, reject or normalize naive datetimes so check-ins are stored as absolute UTC instants, and update `escalate_to_partner` so validation happens before any outbound send using authoritative `TurnContext` metadata for trigger charge and explicit partner-alert requests. Log a distinguished escalation reasoning entry and always use the `escalation` template fallback for the other partner when allowed.

### T3 — Tests (was plan-5 T8)
Update test fakes and focused tests for scheduler, pause/resume, decay, sorting, and crisis behavior: extend `FakePool` narrowly for new scheduler/system_state fields and queries; add or update tests covering weekly summary plus decay, check-in superseding and race protection, late-job recovery, retry cancellation, SKIP LOCKED/concurrent claim behavior, pause symmetry including media paths, resume without backlog replay, deterministic decay thresholds, weighted ordering with null significance, and escalation gate/template fallback.

### T4 — Operational docs (was plan-5 T9)
Document operational choices in repo docs or comments: in-process FastAPI scheduler rationale, horizontal safety via SKIP LOCKED, heartbeat-as-scheduled-job behavior, external/Railway alerting expectations for missing heartbeat, and any weekly-summary timing source chosen for per-user recurrence.

### T5 — Validation (was plan-5 T10)
Run validation and fix until clean: run targeted tests first (`tests/test_decay.py`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_pause_resume.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_agentic.py`, `tests/test_recovery.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py` where present), then full `pytest`. Also write a short throwaway script in `/tmp` that reproduces the highest-risk bug paths from this plan, especially check-in uniqueness and pause media enqueue prevention, run it, confirm the fixes, and delete it before finishing. Do not create additional test files in this final validation task.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Re-running anything plan-5 actually completed (T1–T4, T6, T11). Inspect first; do not duplicate.
- Anything from plan-6 (admin view, onboarding, audit, feedback) — that's a separate plan.
- Anything from plan-7 (evals).

## Success criteria
- `must`: `app/services/system_state.py` exists; `paused_for_user(user_id)` reads from `system_state`; `/pause` and `/resume` literal slash commands halt and resume both partners symmetrically; pause confirmations use `ignore_pause=True` to send through the pause.
- `must`: `schedule_checkin` is transactional under concurrent calls (no double-insert under the partial unique constraint); naive datetimes are rejected or normalized to UTC.
- `must`: `escalate_to_partner` validates the gate (crisis charge OR explicit partner-alert request) BEFORE calling `send_outbound`, using `TurnContext` metadata as the source of truth.
- `must`: `pytest` is green for `tests/test_pause_resume.py` (new), `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_decay.py`, and any pre-existing tests that continue to apply.
- `must`: Operator docs (README or `docs/scheduler.md`) explain the scheduler runtime, SKIP LOCKED safety, heartbeat-as-job, and missing-heartbeat alerting.
- `should`: Concurrent-claim behavior is covered by a test that fires two `ScheduledJobWorker.run_due_once()` calls against the same backlog and asserts no double-firing.
- `info`: Throwaway repro script in `/tmp` was written, run, and deleted (mention in commit/notes).

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] check-in-scheduling: check-in scheduling: one-pending-check-in invariant remains race-prone without a partial unique index or transactional locking strategy. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: weekly summary timing: the spec requires the recurring weekly summary to fire at 09:00 local on the user's configured day, and the plan explicitly asks where that configuration should live. the implementation plan chooses global env defaults plus per-job context overrides, but `/pause` supersedes all pending jobs and `/resume` restores weekly summaries; without a durable per-user schedule field or an explicit rule to read the last superseded weekly job context, resume/startup seeding can silently lose the user's configured day. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: check-in uniqueness: the success criteria require one pending check-in per user, and the current `schedule_checkin` implementation performs a separate `update scheduled_jobs set status='superseded'` followed by `insert into scheduled_jobs`. the plan later says to add db-level coverage, but the migration step only mentions indexes and does not specify a partial unique index such as one pending check-in per user, so two concurrent schedule calls can still race and leave multiple pending check-ins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] edit-handling: edit handling: edit events that reuse the original whatsapp message id can be swallowed by the normal idempotent insert path unless edit detection is explicitly performed before duplicate-message handling. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate: escalation gate: the plan does not define how the write tool receives authoritative trigger charge or explicit-request evidence. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] escalation-gate-ordering: escalation gate ordering: the revised `escalate_to_partner` step sends the partner outbound before the plan text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the plan proposes replacing `hooks.paused_for_user()` with a db-backed implementation and notes that changing call sites to pass `pool` is cleaner. grep shows current direct callers in `messaging.send_outbound`, `inbound.process_inbound`, and `agentic.run_agentic_turn`; tests also monkeypatch a one-argument hook. if the executor chooses the cleaner signature change, all these callers and tests must be updated together, and media handlers still need separate pause coverage as noted in the scope finding. - [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `send_outbound` callers are `agentic.run_agentic_turn`, `write_tools.escalate_to_partner`, and tests, and the function currently accepts only `template_fallback` and `bot_turn_id` keyword arguments. adding an `ignore_pause` keyword is compatible if defaulted to false, but `/pause` confirmation send sites must pass it explicitly or the confirmation rows will be withheld immediately after the global pause flag is set.         Idea:
# Plan 5b — Plan-5 recovery: pause/resume + write-tool tightening + tests + docs + validation

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Pause / Resume", "Crisis Handling" (escalation gate), "Triggers" (scheduled), "Decay" (only as it relates to test coverage), and the relevant parts of "Multi-Message Handling" (media paths under pause).

## Background

Plan-5 (`plan-5-scheduled-jobs-decay-20260430-1337`) was force-proceeded to `done` after codex pre-emptively skipped 5 of 11 tasks in batch 4 onward. The codex executor hallucinated a read-only sandbox and refused before attempting the work. The root cause has been fixed in megaplan (`workers.py` → `_codex_exec_mode_flags` now always returns `--full-auto` for non-execute phases, and execute on macOS already had it). With that fix in place, this plan re-runs the five skipped tasks against the existing repo state.

**The work that landed in plan-5 (do not redo):** migration `0004_plan5_scheduled_jobs.sql`, `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, and the recency-weighted query/sort updates in read tools and hot context.

**The five tasks below were skipped and must land here.** Descriptions are pulled verbatim from plan-5's `finalize.json` so wording matches the original briefing.

## Scope (in)

### T1 — Global pause/resume (was plan-5 T5)
Implement global pause/resume: add `app/services/system_state.py`, make `paused_for_user` DB-backed without breaking all existing callers, add `send_outbound(..., ignore_pause=False)` and use `ignore_pause=True` for pause confirmations, detect literal `/pause` and `/resume` after inbound persistence, supersede all pending user-facing jobs on pause, clear pause and restore weekly summaries on resume, and ensure text, document, voice, and image inbound paths persist but do not enqueue while paused.

### T2 — Write-tool tightening (was plan-5 T7)
Tighten write-tool contracts: keep `schedule_checkin` transactional and compatible with the partial unique constraint, reject or normalize naive datetimes so check-ins are stored as absolute UTC instants, and update `escalate_to_partner` so validation happens before any outbound send using authoritative `TurnContext` metadata for trigger charge and explicit partner-alert requests. Log a distinguished escalation reasoning entry and always use the `escalation` template fallback for the other partner when allowed.

### T3 — Tests (was plan-5 T8)
Update test fakes and focused tests for scheduler, pause/resume, decay, sorting, and crisis behavior: extend `FakePool` narrowly for new scheduler/system_state fields and queries; add or update tests covering weekly summary plus decay, check-in superseding and race protection, late-job recovery, retry cancellation, SKIP LOCKED/concurrent claim behavior, pause symmetry including media paths, resume without backlog replay, deterministic decay thresholds, weighted ordering with null significance, and escalation gate/template fallback.

### T4 — Operational docs (was plan-5 T9)
Document operational choices in repo docs or comments: in-process FastAPI scheduler rationale, horizontal safety via SKIP LOCKED, heartbeat-as-scheduled-job behavior, external/Railway alerting expectations for missing heartbeat, and any weekly-summary timing source chosen for per-user recurrence.

### T5 — Validation (was plan-5 T10)
Run validation and fix until clean: run targeted tests first (`tests/test_decay.py`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_pause_resume.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_agentic.py`, `tests/test_recovery.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py` where present), then full `pytest`. Also write a short throwaway script in `/tmp` that reproduces the highest-risk bug paths from this plan, especially check-in uniqueness and pause media enqueue prevention, run it, confirm the fixes, and delete it before finishing. Do not create additional test files in this final validation task.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Re-running anything plan-5 actually completed (T1–T4, T6, T11). Inspect first; do not duplicate.
- Anything from plan-6 (admin view, onboarding, audit, feedback) — that's a separate plan.
- Anything from plan-7 (evals).

## Success criteria
- `must`: `app/services/system_state.py` exists; `paused_for_user(user_id)` reads from `system_state`; `/pause` and `/resume` literal slash commands halt and resume both partners symmetrically; pause confirmations use `ignore_pause=True` to send through the pause.
- `must`: `schedule_checkin` is transactional under concurrent calls (no double-insert under the partial unique constraint); naive datetimes are rejected or normalized to UTC.
- `must`: `escalate_to_partner` validates the gate (crisis charge OR explicit partner-alert request) BEFORE calling `send_outbound`, using `TurnContext` metadata as the source of truth.
- `must`: `pytest` is green for `tests/test_pause_resume.py` (new), `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_decay.py`, and any pre-existing tests that continue to apply.
- `must`: Operator docs (README or `docs/scheduler.md`) explain the scheduler runtime, SKIP LOCKED safety, heartbeat-as-job, and missing-heartbeat alerting.
- `should`: Concurrent-claim behavior is covered by a test that fires two `ScheduledJobWorker.run_due_once()` calls against the same backlog and asserts no double-firing.
- `info`: Throwaway repro script in `/tmp` was written, run, and deleted (mention in commit/notes).

## Notes
- This is light robustness — one critique + revise iteration, then finalize → execute. No gate, no review.
- Codex now runs with `--full-auto` for all phases on this machine (megaplan fix landed). If a task still hits a "read-only" hallucination, retry once with `--fresh`. Do not skip pre-emptively.
- Read plan-5's `plan_v1.md` and `finalize.json` for design context; do not reinvent decisions plan-5 already made.
- All five tasks should land in a single execute pass — they're not deeply interlocked, but they share `FakePool` extensions and `system_state` import surface, so coordinated edits are easier than serial.

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
# Plan 5b — Plan-5 recovery: pause/resume + write-tool tightening + tests + docs + validation

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Pause / Resume", "Crisis Handling" (escalation gate), "Triggers" (scheduled), "Decay" (only as it relates to test coverage), and the relevant parts of "Multi-Message Handling" (media paths under pause).

## Background

Plan-5 (`plan-5-scheduled-jobs-decay-20260430-1337`) was force-proceeded to `done` after codex pre-emptively skipped 5 of 11 tasks in batch 4 onward. The codex executor hallucinated a read-only sandbox and refused before attempting the work. The root cause has been fixed in megaplan (`workers.py` → `_codex_exec_mode_flags` now always returns `--full-auto` for non-execute phases, and execute on macOS already had it). With that fix in place, this plan re-runs the five skipped tasks against the existing repo state.

**The work that landed in plan-5 (do not redo):** migration `0004_plan5_scheduled_jobs.sql`, `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, and the recency-weighted query/sort updates in read tools and hot context.

**The five tasks below were skipped and must land here.** Descriptions are pulled verbatim from plan-5's `finalize.json` so wording matches the original briefing.

## Scope (in)

### T1 — Global pause/resume (was plan-5 T5)
Implement global pause/resume: add `app/services/system_state.py`, make `paused_for_user` DB-backed without breaking all existing callers, add `send_outbound(..., ignore_pause=False)` and use `ignore_pause=True` for pause confirmations, detect literal `/pause` and `/resume` after inbound persistence, supersede all pending user-facing jobs on pause, clear pause and restore weekly summaries on resume, and ensure text, document, voice, and image inbound paths persist but do not enqueue while paused.

### T2 — Write-tool tightening (was plan-5 T7)
Tighten write-tool contracts: keep `schedule_checkin` transactional and compatible with the partial unique constraint, reject or normalize naive datetimes so check-ins are stored as absolute UTC instants, and update `escalate_to_partner` so validation happens before any outbound send using authoritative `TurnContext` metadata for trigger charge and explicit partner-alert requests. Log a distinguished escalation reasoning entry and always use the `escalation` template fallback for the other partner when allowed.

### T3 — Tests (was plan-5 T8)
Update test fakes and focused tests for scheduler, pause/resume, decay, sorting, and crisis behavior: extend `FakePool` narrowly for new scheduler/system_state fields and queries; add or update tests covering weekly summary plus decay, check-in superseding and race protection, late-job recovery, retry cancellation, SKIP LOCKED/concurrent claim behavior, pause symmetry including media paths, resume without backlog replay, deterministic decay thresholds, weighted ordering with null significance, and escalation gate/template fallback.

### T4 — Operational docs (was plan-5 T9)
Document operational choices in repo docs or comments: in-process FastAPI scheduler rationale, horizontal safety via SKIP LOCKED, heartbeat-as-scheduled-job behavior, external/Railway alerting expectations for missing heartbeat, and any weekly-summary timing source chosen for per-user recurrence.

### T5 — Validation (was plan-5 T10)
Run validation and fix until clean: run targeted tests first (`tests/test_decay.py`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_pause_resume.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_agentic.py`, `tests/test_recovery.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py` where present), then full `pytest`. Also write a short throwaway script in `/tmp` that reproduces the highest-risk bug paths from this plan, especially check-in uniqueness and pause media enqueue prevention, run it, confirm the fixes, and delete it before finishing. Do not create additional test files in this final validation task.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Re-running anything plan-5 actually completed (T1–T4, T6, T11). Inspect first; do not duplicate.
- Anything from plan-6 (admin view, onboarding, audit, feedback) — that's a separate plan.
- Anything from plan-7 (evals).

## Success criteria
- `must`: `app/services/system_state.py` exists; `paused_for_user(user_id)` reads from `system_state`; `/pause` and `/resume` literal slash commands halt and resume both partners symmetrically; pause confirmations use `ignore_pause=True` to send through the pause.
- `must`: `schedule_checkin` is transactional under concurrent calls (no double-insert under the partial unique constraint); naive datetimes are rejected or normalized to UTC.
- `must`: `escalate_to_partner` validates the gate (crisis charge OR explicit partner-alert request) BEFORE calling `send_outbound`, using `TurnContext` metadata as the source of truth.
- `must`: `pytest` is green for `tests/test_pause_resume.py` (new), `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_decay.py`, and any pre-existing tests that continue to apply.
- `must`: Operator docs (README or `docs/scheduler.md`) explain the scheduler runtime, SKIP LOCKED safety, heartbeat-as-job, and missing-heartbeat alerting.
- `should`: Concurrent-claim behavior is covered by a test that fires two `ScheduledJobWorker.run_due_once()` calls against the same backlog and asserts no double-firing.
- `info`: Throwaway repro script in `/tmp` was written, run, and deleted (mention in commit/notes).

## Notes
- This is light robustness — one critique + revise iteration, then finalize → execute. No gate, no review.
- Codex now runs with `--full-auto` for all phases on this machine (megaplan fix landed). If a task still hits a "read-only" hallucination, retry once with `--fresh`. Do not skip pre-emptively.
- Read plan-5's `plan_v1.md` and `finalize.json` for design context; do not reinvent decisions plan-5 already made.
- All five tasks should land in a single execute pass — they're not deeply interlocked, but they share `FakePool` extensions and `system_state` import surface, so coordinated edits are easier than serial.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] check-in-scheduling: check-in scheduling: one-pending-check-in invariant remains race-prone without a partial unique index or transactional locking strategy. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: weekly summary timing: the spec requires the recurring weekly summary to fire at 09:00 local on the user's configured day, and the plan explicitly asks where that configuration should live. the implementation plan chooses global env defaults plus per-job context overrides, but `/pause` supersedes all pending jobs and `/resume` restores weekly summaries; without a durable per-user schedule field or an explicit rule to read the last superseded weekly job context, resume/startup seeding can silently lose the user's configured day. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: check-in uniqueness: the success criteria require one pending check-in per user, and the current `schedule_checkin` implementation performs a separate `update scheduled_jobs set status='superseded'` followed by `insert into scheduled_jobs`. the plan later says to add db-level coverage, but the migration step only mentions indexes and does not specify a partial unique index such as one pending check-in per user, so two concurrent schedule calls can still race and leave multiple pending check-ins. {
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/docs/scheduler.md\n+# Scheduler Operations\n+\n+The scheduler runs in-process with the FastAPI app. This keeps Railway\n+deployment simple: one service owns WhatsApp webhooks, agentic turns, and due\n+scheduled jobs, all backed by the same Postgres tables. Disable it with the\n+existing `SCHEDULER_ENABLED=false` setting only when another deployed app\n+instance is intentionally taking over scheduler work.\n+\n+## Runtime Model\n+\n+`app.main` starts `ScheduledJobWorker.run_forever()` during FastAPI lifespan\n+startup when `SCHEDULER_ENABLED` is true. Each tick polls every\n+`SCHEDULER_POLL_INTERVAL_S` seconds and claims up to `SCHEDULER_BATCH_SIZE`\n+pending rows from `scheduled_jobs`.\n+\n+The claim query uses `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` inside a single\n+`UPDATE ... RETURNING` statement. That is the horizontal-safety boundary:\n+multiple Railway instances may tick at the same time, but a due job row can be\n+claimed by only one worker. If a worker dies after claiming a row, startup\n+recovery clears stale `claimed_at` state for recent due jobs, marks 1-24 hour\n+late jobs as delayed, and cancels jobs more than 24 hours stale.\n+\n+When global pause is enabled, the worker claims only `heartbeat` jobs. User\n+facing jobs remain pending unless `/pause` explicitly supersedes them.\n+\n+## Heartbeat\n+\n+Heartbeat is a `scheduled_jobs` row of type `heartbeat`, not a separate timer.\n+When a heartbeat job fires, the worker writes the normal scheduler heartbeat log\n+line and seeds the next heartbeat using `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_HOURS`. The worker\n+keeps heartbeat active during global pause because it is operational telemetry,\n+not mediation.\n+\n+Alerting for missing heartbeat is external to the app. On Railway, configure a\n+log-based or cron-style monitor that expects a scheduler heartbeat log within\n+the `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_HOURS` cadence plus deployment slack. Alert if the log\n+goes missing while `/health` still reports healthy, because that indicates the\n+web process is up but scheduler work may not be running.\n+\n+## Weekly Summaries\n+\n+Weekly summaries are seeded on startup and after `/resume` from durable per-user\n+fields:\n+\n+- `users.weekly_summary_day`\n+- `users.weekly_summary_time`\n+- `users.timezone`\n+\n+The recurrence calculation converts the configured local day and time to an\n+absolute UTC `scheduled_for` instant. The default configuration values\n+`WEEKLY_SUMMARY_DEFAULT_DAY` and `WEEKLY_SUMMARY_DEFAULT_TIME` are only defaults\n+for user rows; recurrence should not depend on a superseded pending job's JSON\n+context.\n+\n+## Pause And Resume\n+\n+`/pause` sets `system_state.global_pause`, supersedes pending user-facing jobs,\n+and preserves heartbeat jobs. `/resume` clears the global pause and reseeds\n+weekly summaries from the durable user fields above. Stored paused inbound\n+messages are not replayed automatically.\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/README.md\n@@\n See [`docs/ops.md`](docs/ops.md) for uptime monitoring, spend-counter location,\n-and rotation expectations.\n+rotation expectations, and [`docs/scheduler.md`](docs/scheduler.md) for\n+scheduler runtime ownership, heartbeat, pause/resume, and weekly-summary\n+operations.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

# Plan 5b — Plan-5 recovery: pause/resume + write-tool tightening + tests + docs + validation

Authoritative spec: `mediator-bot-spec.md`. Read in prep, focusing on: "Pause / Resume", "Crisis Handling" (escalation gate), "Triggers" (scheduled), "Decay" (only as it relates to test coverage), and the relevant parts of "Multi-Message Handling" (media paths under pause).

## Background

Plan-5 (`plan-5-scheduled-jobs-decay-20260430-1337`) was force-proceeded to `done` after codex pre-emptively skipped 5 of 11 tasks in batch 4 onward. The codex executor hallucinated a read-only sandbox and refused before attempting the work. The root cause has been fixed in megaplan (`workers.py` → `_codex_exec_mode_flags` now always returns `--full-auto` for non-execute phases, and execute on macOS already had it). With that fix in place, this plan re-runs the five skipped tasks against the existing repo state.

**The work that landed in plan-5 (do not redo):** migration `0004_plan5_scheduled_jobs.sql`, `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, and the recency-weighted query/sort updates in read tools and hot context.

**The five tasks below were skipped and must land here.** Descriptions are pulled verbatim from plan-5's `finalize.json` so wording matches the original briefing.

## Scope (in)

### T1 — Global pause/resume (was plan-5 T5)
Implement global pause/resume: add `app/services/system_state.py`, make `paused_for_user` DB-backed without breaking all existing callers, add `send_outbound(..., ignore_pause=False)` and use `ignore_pause=True` for pause confirmations, detect literal `/pause` and `/resume` after inbound persistence, supersede all pending user-facing jobs on pause, clear pause and restore weekly summaries on resume, and ensure text, document, voice, and image inbound paths persist but do not enqueue while paused.

### T2 — Write-tool tightening (was plan-5 T7)
Tighten write-tool contracts: keep `schedule_checkin` transactional and compatible with the partial unique constraint, reject or normalize naive datetimes so check-ins are stored as absolute UTC instants, and update `escalate_to_partner` so validation happens before any outbound send using authoritative `TurnContext` metadata for trigger charge and explicit partner-alert requests. Log a distinguished escalation reasoning entry and always use the `escalation` template fallback for the other partner when allowed.

### T3 — Tests (was plan-5 T8)
Update test fakes and focused tests for scheduler, pause/resume, decay, sorting, and crisis behavior: extend `FakePool` narrowly for new scheduler/system_state fields and queries; add or update tests covering weekly summary plus decay, check-in superseding and race protection, late-job recovery, retry cancellation, SKIP LOCKED/concurrent claim behavior, pause symmetry including media paths, resume without backlog replay, deterministic decay thresholds, weighted ordering with null significance, and escalation gate/template fallback.

### T4 — Operational docs (was plan-5 T9)
Document operational choices in repo docs or comments: in-process FastAPI scheduler rationale, horizontal safety via SKIP LOCKED, heartbeat-as-scheduled-job behavior, external/Railway alerting expectations for missing heartbeat, and any weekly-summary timing source chosen for per-user recurrence.

### T5 — Validation (was plan-5 T10)
Run validation and fix until clean: run targeted tests first (`tests/test_decay.py`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_pause_resume.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_agentic.py`, `tests/test_recovery.py`, `tests/test_send_outbound.py` where present), then full `pytest`. Also write a short throwaway script in `/tmp` that reproduces the highest-risk bug paths from this plan, especially check-in uniqueness and pause media enqueue prevention, run it, confirm the fixes, and delete it before finishing. Do not create additional test files in this final validation task.

## Scope (out, deliberately)
- Re-running anything plan-5 actually completed (T1–T4, T6, T11). Inspect first; do not duplicate.
- Anything from plan-6 (admin view, onboarding, audit, feedback) — that's a separate plan.
- Anything from plan-7 (evals).

## Success criteria
- `must`: `app/services/system_state.py` exists; `paused_for_user(user_id)` reads from `system_state`; `/pause` and `/resume` literal slash commands halt and resume both partners symmetrically; pause confirmations use `ignore_pause=True` to send through the pause.
- `must`: `schedule_checkin` is transactional under concurrent calls (no double-insert under the partial unique constraint); naive datetimes are rejected or normalized to UTC.
- `must`: `escalate_to_partner` validates the gate (crisis charge OR explicit partner-alert request) BEFORE calling `send_outbound`, using `TurnContext` metadata as the source of truth.
- `must`: `pytest` is green for `tests/test_pause_resume.py` (new), `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `tests/test_decay.py`, and any pre-existing tests that continue to apply.
- `must`: Operator docs (README or `docs/scheduler.md`) explain the scheduler runtime, SKIP LOCKED safety, heartbeat-as-job, and missing-heartbeat alerting.
- `should`: Concurrent-claim behavior is covered by a test that fires two `ScheduledJobWorker.run_due_once()` calls against the same backlog and asserts no double-firing.
- `info`: Throwaway repro script in `/tmp` was written, run, and deleted (mention in commit/notes).

## Notes

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: debouncer callback: revised step 8 defines `burstcoalescer.add(user_id, message_id)` but says expiry calls `on_burst_complete(message_ids, user)`. i found no existing user model or repository helper in the current codebase, and the plan does not say whether the coalescer stores the user object, fetches it from the pool, or receives it in `add`, so the callback cannot reliably supply the `user` argument required by `run_agentic_turn` as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: edit idempotency: revised step 9 says the edit fixture reuses the original whatsapp message id, while revised step 10's normal message flow does `insert ... on conflict (whatsapp_message_id) do nothing` and treats no returned id as a redelivery. unless edit detection runs before the generic idempotent insert path, a real edit will be classified as a duplicate and skipped instead of updating `edit_history`, but the plan does not explicitly require that ordering. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `escalate_to_partner` sequence. step 5 constructs the `templatecall` and awaits `send_outbound(...)` before the text says it enforces the crisis/explicit-request gate, so an implementation following the plan literally could send an unauthorized escalation before rejecting the tool call. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the revised `tool_call_count` expectations against the spec. the plan's e2e criterion expects `tool_call_count == 3` because only write calls are counted, but the spec says `bot_turns` records tool count for admin visibility into pathological loops; excluding phase a read calls hides most loop activity. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: query-level decay: `observations.significance` is nullable in `tool_schemas.py` and in current tests for failed scoring, and `scoring.rescore_observations` can leave it null after a failed re-score. the plan's sql function `recency_weighted_score(significance, last_reinforced_at, created_at)` does not specify null handling or `nulls last`; in postgres, ordering that nullable expression `desc` can surface null-scored failed observations before valid scored observations unless the executor adds `nulls last` or `coalesce`. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: escalation gate metadata: the plan says `escalate_to_partner` should accept `is_crisis=true` only when the current trigger charge is actually `crisis`, but the existing write-tool context only has `turncontext(triggering_message_ids, user, partner, phase)` and the charge is currently computed locally inside `agentic.run_agentic_turn` from hot context. the plan does not explicitly add that charge or an explicit-request marker to `turncontext` or the tool-call validator, so the proposed guard has no reliable data path in the current code shape. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] check-in-scheduling: check-in scheduling: one-pending-check-in invariant remains race-prone without a partial unique index or transactional locking strategy. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] debouncer: debouncer: coalescer cannot satisfy the `run_agentic_turn(message_ids, user)` callback contract from an `add(user_id, message_id)` api unless it stores or resolves the user object. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the search-before-write requirement against the new phase split. because phase b exposes only write/schedule tools, the model cannot recover by calling `get_memories`, `get_observations`, or `list_themes` if it realizes during phase b that it lacks enough read context to safely add versus update; this creates tension with the explicit agentic-search dedup requirement. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: weekly summary timing: the spec requires the recurring weekly summary to fire at 09:00 local on the user's configured day, and the plan explicitly asks where that configuration should live. the implementation plan chooses global env defaults plus per-job context overrides, but `/pause` supersedes all pending jobs and `/resume` restores weekly summaries; without a durable per-user schedule field or an explicit rule to read the last superseded weekly job context, resume/startup seeding can silently lose the user's configured day. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: recovery/coalescer api mismatch: revised step 14 says recovery should re-add a whole `triggering_message_ids` list as one burst, but revised step 8 only specifies `add(user_id, message_id)` for one message at a time. calling `add` repeatedly can reset timers or merge with live inbound state, so the plan needs either an explicit `add_burst(user_id, ids)`/`fire(ids)` path or clear semantics for preserving a recovered burst as a single unit. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: indexing claim: revised step 2 says the new gin index supports recovery's `messages.id = any(triggering_message_ids)` lookup, but postgres gin indexes on arrays are normally used by array operators such as `@>`, `&&`, or `<@`, not by scalar `= any(array)` in a correlated subquery. the executor should either use an indexable predicate like `triggering_message_ids @> array[messages.id]` or drop the claim that this query is gin-backed. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked audit support around `prompt_snapshot`. the revised step 8 still opens `bot_turns` with `prompt_snapshot` before building hot context, while the spec's worked example calls for `<full prompt>` after hot context is loaded; unless the executor reorders this, the stored prompt snapshot will omit the per-turn hot context actually injected into the llm. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: check-in uniqueness: the success criteria require one pending check-in per user, and the current `schedule_checkin` implementation performs a separate `update scheduled_jobs set status='superseded'` followed by `insert into scheduled_jobs`. the plan later says to add db-level coverage, but the migration step only mentions indexes and does not specify a partial unique index such as one pending check-in per user, so two concurrent schedule calls can still race and leave multiple pending check-ins. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

- [DEBT] escalation-gate: escalation gate: the plan does not define how the write tool receives authoritative trigger charge or explicit-request evidence. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
